ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u1	Good morning. The session is open. The agenda is published in the Official Bulletin of the Parliament of Galicia, so we do not proceed to its reading. No inassistances have been justified. [[One person from the House's cleaning services comes to disinfect the floor.]] We went into the agenda with the first point. Debates and votes on the ceiling on non-financial expenditure of the Autonomous Community of Galicia for the year 2021, as referred to in Article 12 of Law 2/2011 of 16 June 2011 on budgetary discipline and financial sustainability
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u2	All this was because the Council of Galicia, on 29 October 2020, agreed on the ceiling on non-financial expenditure of the general budgets of the Autonomous Community of Galicia for the year 2021, which is set at EUR 11,664 million. In accordance with Article 12 of Law 2/2011 of 16 June, on budgetary discipline and financial sustainability, it was forwarded to this Chamber for approval, together with the report of the Financial and Fiscal Strategy, referred to in Article 12.1 of that Act. Before we proceed to the vote, the Government has a fifteen-minute round for its presentation. And then there will be a round for each of the parliamentary groups for a time of ten minutes. We therefore begin with the presentation by Mr. Valeriano Martínez García.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u3	Thank you very much, President. Good morning, gentlemen. I am here today before Parliament to present the non-financial expenditure limit for the year 2021, which was recently adopted by the Council of the Council of Galicia in accordance with Article 12 of the Law on budgetary discipline and financial sustainability. One more year I want to put in value that the German Government has been pioneering in the adoption by law of this instrument, and this is already the tenth year in which it presents it to the House. In addition, with the aim of promoting maximum transparency of the legislation adopted a decade ago, it was not only that the Executive had to approve the spending ceiling, but that he had to submit it to the consideration of the Autonomic Parliament to request permission to this non-financial expenditure limit, which is what we are now dealing with. Ladies and gentlemen, firstly, I would like to explain why for the third consecutive year the expenditure ceiling comes to Parliament with a delay on the normal and usual budgetary cycle. A delay not attributable to the Greek Government, as I have now exposed. The Board of Directors had to approve this non-financial spending limit later than usual because the first data to be available for processing did not reach us until just a month ago. And we also received incomplete data, because the information as relevant as the salary of officials or European funds did not have it until the same week as we approved it on 29 October. In view of this scenario, from the Business Council and Public Administration, we want to accelerate work more than ever with a goal: to compensate for a delay of more than two months over a normal budget cycle. We have already commented that this year we understand that there could be some delay in communicating deliveries to the account and the public deficit schedule, but what is not reasonable is that since February the Fiscal and Financial Policy Council was not convened until October 5. In addition, this delay, which could be an exception this year, has become a habit of the current Central Government, which in both 2019 and 2020 as well as 2021 has always breached legal deadlines. In the face of this circumstance, since the Greek Government — I insist — we work with the greatest possible speed and accumulated experience to first approve the spending ceiling — twelve days ago — and, secondly, to be able to approve the budgets before the end of this month of November. We want to have the budgets of 2021 in force before, because it's being a fundamental tool for the fight against the pandemic. And we want next year to be also an instrument for the fight against the pandemic — which I was referring to a moment ago — but also of economic reactivation. And the second important question is that the spending ceiling we are debating today is the highest in the historical series, which we started a decade ago, because we are in an exceptional situation. What do we intend? Face with guarantees of the health pandemic, reactivate the economy and care for families and businesses that are suffering from the economic crisis. Ladies and gentlemen, we must take into account the context in which this expenditure ceiling is discussed and why it is an exceptional expenditure ceiling. And the reason is that after five years of strong growth and job creation, unfortunately, as of March this year, the pandemic abruptly trumped that good evolution. The first thing we did was an update on the macroeconomic framework by 2020, since — as I have just said — the pandemic has completely changed the stage. If we have always made prudent and realistic estimates, this is more relevant this time. According to our forecasts, the impact on the Greek economy of the Covid-19 will lead to a fall in GDP of 9.7% in real terms, which, if well is smaller than the Spanish average, will be very important. In terms of employment, the impact of the pandemic will lead to a fall in employment of 10.6%, measured at full-time equivalent jobs, with which the average rate of unemployment in this exercise is estimated to be 12.3%, also lower, significantly lower than in Spain as a whole. Once this year's macroeconomic framework is adapted to the pandemic situation, the next step in developing the non-financial expenditure ceiling for 2021 was also to carry out realistic macroeconomic framework estimates for the next year. In this sense, obviously, there is a high uncertainty about the evolution and intensity of the pandemic, which will undoubtedly impact on the speed of recovery of the economy. With this conditionant and with all caution, we believe that in 2021 Galicia could have a real GDP increase of 8%. This recovery will also increase full-time equivalent jobs by 7%, with the average unemployment rate estimated by 2021 at 12.9%. I explained below how we calculated the income. And the first thing I want to point out is that we have some resources that we can consider ordinary and others that we qualify as extraordinary. Starting with ordinary income, it is customary for those from the autonomic financing system to represent almost 80% of the total non-financial resources, but in this exceptional expenditure ceiling, on account of the extraordinary funds planned, it is only 67%. In 2021 total resources from the autonomous financing system will be EUR 7,949 million and will decrease by 1.6 % compared to this year 2020. As regards the ordinary finalist transfers of other administrations, they will remain at EUR 1,176.5 million. In particular, as far as European funds are concerned, we estimate a forecast that is very similar to this exercise, with a slight increase of 0.7%, up to EUR 629.2 million, and with which we close the budgeting of the ERDF and the European Social Fund under 2014-2020, which is currently in force. As for final government funding, it lowers its estimate at 1.6%, mainly by reducing income from employment end-users to 6.9% and by the expected fall of 9.1% in the International Compensation Fund, which remains at EUR 38.5 million. On the other hand, the non-financial own resources of the Autonomous Community, where we include taxes administered by the Union, own taxes, public prices and capital income, we estimate that their amount will be reduced by 7.6%. Logically, its evolution depends to a large extent on the main tax yields: the tax on documented heritage transmissions and legal acts, the tax on inheritances and donations and the tax on property, since these three taxes represent more than half of this departure. The decrease is mainly due to the anticipated evolution in the tax on capital transfers and documented legal acts, so that next year the rights expected to be recognised by that tax are reduced by 18.4% in relation to the current budget. But it must be borne in mind that this year's forecast is that the recognized rights are only 80% of the initial forecast, as a result, also obviously, of the paralysis that has suffered the economy for much of the year and that this tax is very tied to the economic cycle. In addition, ordinary resources for the next exercise will amount to EUR 9,864.2 million, after including what is called "national accounting adjustments", in this negative case, amounting to EUR 33 million. In addition, as I said at the outset, this spending ceiling is exceptional because it has extraordinary resources. And the government's political decision is to make the most of the possibilities that we have to achieve these extraordinary resources. On the one hand, we include Galicia's share of the extraordinary transfer of the State to the autonomous communities, of an overall amount of EUR 13,456 million, as announced by the Council of Ministers on October 6, and as corroborated by the 2021 budget plan sent to Brussels on October 15. In view of the lack of realisation by the Ministry of Finance of how the said resources will be distributed among the autonomous communities, our estimate is EUR 684.9 million as a result of applying 1.1% to GDP estimated for Galicia in 2021. And, on the other hand, we collect the REACT-EU resources, Aid to Recovery for Cohesion and the Territories of Europe, which are an initiative that pursues and expands measures to respond to the crisis and repair of it. The Kingdom of Spain has resources amounting to 12,400 million euros, of which 10,000 million will be distributed among the autonomous communities; 8,000 million in 2021 and 2,000 million in 2022, as agreed by the Fiscal and Financial Policy Council. From these extraordinary European funds we included a prudent forecast for Galicia of 430 million. With regard to the public deficit, as a result of the extraordinary situation this year, the Council of Ministers decided to suspend fiscal rules during the years 2020 and 2021, although reference rates have been established which we are going to follow in Galicia. Thus, we decided to use the public deficit of 1.1%, another 684.9 million euros, as much as possible at the reference rate targeted by the Central Government. And we made this decision after we have in practice completed fiscal consolidation and being the Community which has increased its indebtedness in the last decade. Precisely the responsible indebtedness carried out since 2009 puts Galicia in a better position for power now, which is necessary, increasing it. With this policy of obeisance, the Greeks avoid being indebted to EUR 5,654 million in the last decade. [[Applause]] And the result is that every Greek and Galician owes 2,095 euros less than the average of the rest of Spanish. In addition, thanks to the confidence that Galicia reaches investors, in 2019 we were one of the few autonomous communities that returned to the financial markets to take advantage of the conditions it offers, with abundant liquidity, high demand and historically low interest rates. At the end of 2019, in Galicia we even formalized the first debt issue with negative profitability in the history of autonomy. And a few weeks ago, we just confirmed the second one. In addition, this year 2020 we also formalise a release of sustainable bonds, which are entirely targeted at social and environmental investments. The result is that Galicia is in a better position than other public administrations to deal with the health, economic and social crisis linked to the pandemic. With these revenues, by next year we will have a spending limit of 11,664 million euros. And from this amount, 9,864.2 million will be income that we can consider ordinary. And almost EUR 8 billion that we can call extraordinary because of this exceptional situation that we have to face. With these forecasts, we face an exceptional, prudent and rigorous expenditure ceiling for 2021. A non-financial expenditure ceiling, which, as I say, amounts to EUR 11,664 million, means a 14.9 % increase in the budget this year. It's the biggest expenditure ceiling since this instrument exists in Galicia, implanted a decade ago, but — I repeat — we are in an exceptional situation. Of the 11,664 million, 11,012 million are non-financial revenues, 685 million come from the deficit target and 33 million are negative adjustments in national accounts. This 14.9 % increase in the non-financial expenditure ceiling for the next exercise will allow...
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u4	Finish, please.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u5	...that we increase the spending capacity in a total of 1,516 million euros. Ladies and gentlemen, I call on all parliamentary groups to support this extraordinary expenditure ceiling, because I understand that it is the financial framework necessary to deal with the exceptional moment we are living in. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u19	People's Parliamentary Group, Mr Puy.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u20	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Counselor, ladies. In the face of an extraordinary situation, the government also brings us in the financial sector is an extraordinary expenditure ceiling. We went up from EUR 10.148 million last year — 300 million the spending ceiling of the previous year — to a spending limit of EUR 11.664 million; that is, EUR 1.516 million over the year 2020, an increase of almost 15%. We always give this debate the transcendence it deserves; it is the tenth time, indeed, that we celebrate it, but it takes place for the first time in a context of suspension of the rules of financial stability. And that's why this debate is of particular importance added. First, because it reflects the commitment to the transparency of the Government of the United States — and remember that this is one of the few parliaments in which the ceiling for self-sustainment is discussed, because in most of them this debate is absent from the chambers — transparency on what is the government's budgetary policy. But secondly, because it also complies with the Greek Act of financial stability and sustainability it allows us to maintain the medium and long-term commitment to financial stability, consistent with the objectives of the European Union and also with the economic orthodoxy that it follows, which is basically based on a long-term Keynesian balance, over the cycle, and around a social and market economy that is also generating the most prosperity on the European continent. Counsellor, we will obviously support the expenditure ceiling, for three reasons. First of all, because we understand that it is in line with the actions and recommendations of all experts and authorities, both of the Greeks, Spanish and European and international. Secondly, because it is based, with all cautions, on quite realistic forecasts of macroeconomic evolution, of course much more realistic than those of the Spanish Government. And thirdly, because we believe that approving the spending ceiling will contribute to increasing the certainty where we live in a deep uncertainty in an environment where both administration and administration will thus have more certainty that they would otherwise not have a spending roof. The first reason, therefore, is that the expenditure ceiling goes in the path of the right policies, according to all experts, including the Greeks, Spanish, Europeans and international forums. We are in a clear situation of economic pressure which is overcome by an unexpected health crisis, which causes in the economy a drop in both supply and aggregate demand, and what we are giving, since the Galician Union and with the support of the People's Group, an economic policy response based, first, on urgent financing of emergencies, both health and economic, and aid to the most affected productive sectors, as well as in the social sphere, through mechanisms such as direct transfers, reductions in tax charges at the moment and speeding up payment to suppliers. Secondly, when we have the vaccine — to see if it is true that it is going ahead — in the second phase of the pandemic, we will have to push the transformation with more selective support to those sectors that will not only allow a short-term crisis to be overcome, but will ensure greater resilience and prosperity of the Greek economy, moving forward in reducing emissions, in digital infrastructures, of course in health infrastructures. For that, I think that Junta is already laying the first foundations, restarting the ongoing projects before anyone else, accelerating the investment projects that were already in portfolios and also moving forward new projects for society to postcrisis. For in the context of the pandemic crisis, if everyone recommends that this is what needs to be done, we are in the first stage and this expenditure ceiling meets these needs. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u43	The debate on this point has ended. We continue with the agenda, which is now the legislative texts. Comprehensive discussion of the Galician Continental Fisheries Act
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u44	Amendments have been tabled to the entire Socialist Parliamentary Group and to the Parliamentary Group of the Galician Nationalist Block. For the introduction of the bill, Mrs. Ánggeles Vázquez Mejuto has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u45	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, let us bring to this House today the draft of which will be the future Continental Fisheries Act of Galicia; a text which now begins its parliamentary process, which will replace the law of 1992 - three decades of the vigency of a law - and which is now necessary to adapt and modernize. Fishing is a sports activity that gives us the opportunity to be in touch with nature. It's an activity that has many advantages linked: the knowledge it offers of our natural environment, the behavior of the species and also its capacity as a dynamizer of the environment and nature tourism. From the Council of Galicia we know that knowledge and the value of our territory, our natural environment and biodiversity are very important steps towards its protection, so our goal is to reconcile sport and recreation with the conservation of nature and biodiversity. The text we are going to discuss today is based on the importance of fishing in Galicia; a community in which we have 40,000 fishermen, we manage 150,000 fishing permits a year and we have 160 skilled annual fishing days. We are faced with an activity which currently concentrates at 14,043 kilometers from the rivers of Greece, of which are of free water 11,778, which is now 83%; of raw water, 1,114 kilometers, 7%; and fishing fences, 1,551 kilometers, 10%. In Galicia we also have 66 partners in river fishing, 24 in the province of Coruña, 21 in Lugo, 9 in Ourense and 12 in the province of Pontevedra. Of these, 59 have an agreement with the Environmental Council, i.e. 98% of the entities. This new Continental Fisheries Act will be the instrument that enables us to take care of what we already have and move forward in four objectives: to ensure generational remittance and to promote education and awareness of the younger; to promote sustainable and environmentally friendly fishing; to place fishing as a tool for tourism, economic and social development in our rural environment; and to provide us with a new and strategic planning of fisheries management that enables us to value natural fish resources. In the processing of the text we seek the greatest possible consensus and involve all agents, so we consult a total of 47 agencies. And with this new law we will continue to advance in the renewal of the regulatory framework of the natural heritage area, which has had two outstanding features in the last three years: on the one hand, the Act of Protection and Welfare of Pets in Galicia, of which we are currently working on its regulation, and the Law of Natural Heritage, adopted in 2019. I will now highlight the main developments in this new legislative text that we propose for fishing. As I said, one of the main objectives of this law is to guarantee the necessary generational relief in this activity. In this sense, it is important to highlight an important fact, and is that the current average age of fishermen in Galicia is between 50 and 70 years old, although it is true that licences for under 20 years have increased by 3 points since 2010. That's the line we have to follow and what we must conquer, since we are also aware that encouraging contact with young people and girls with nature is always a very positive aspect. We believe that the way in which we must go to achieve this must be based on awareness, training and the promotion of sporting and environmental values associated with this activity among the younger. For that reason, one of the new features of the legislative text is the creation of river schools for the first time in Galicia; schools that will aim to involve and educate smaller ones in the conservation of continental aquatic ecosystems and which will be developed through the regulation of the law, but which can be implemented first through agreements with collaborative entities and with the Galician Fisheries Federation. These schools are already being a success in other autonomous communities, as is the case with Castile and León, and through them young and young people come into contact with nature, respect and also protect their surroundings and in them, through theory, but also through practice, they will learn not only the techniques of fishing, but will acquire knowledge of other kinds, such as flora, fauna and climate. In this way, we are committed to the promotion and training, as well as to education and collaboration with the Welsh strategy of environmental education, providing training through basic principles and always going from the hands of the newer. In addition, in this same line of involving young people and girls in fishing, we also take measures concerning licences. Specifically, boys up to 14 years old can't practice fishing without a license, as long as they're accompanied by an adult. All minors will not have to pay the fees for obtaining a licence and will also enjoy free permits for the practice of fishing without death. The second objective of this text is to continue the bet on sustainable and environmentally friendly fishing. For this reason, the law includes several measures aimed at promoting the progressive involvement of deathless fishing in our autonomous community. To give you the knowledge and drive of this mode among the younger, we've made new settings. Currently in Galicia we have 143 tranches enabled to fish without death, which account for a total of 341 kilometers. What we want is that we continue to coexist the traditional fishing model rooted in our community, that is, extractive, with deathless fishing, and seek a greater balance between the two, just as it is happening in the rest of the state and in other countries around us. For this purpose, the law includes measures such as promotion and dissemination by partner fishing entities, specific training programmes and the freeness of deathless fishing permits for all minors. Through this law we also want to give special protection to traditional fishing techniques, which are representative of culture, but also of the traditional way of life in Galicia. Among them are the fishing grounds of the Ulla River, the estates of the Tea River for the fishing of the lamprea or the canaries of Portomarín. Our goal is to ensure its transmission, promotion and value. The future continental fishing law also seeks to promote the tourism, economic and social development of the natural environment, and to do so in different ways that allow fishing to be a sustainable tourism engine in our society. And for this we think of measures such as those which are reflected in the law: the granting of fish stocks in cours and ponds to tourist companies, the reservation of specific permits for tourist entities, the exemption of license rates over the age of 65 and the simplification and axillization of the processing of minor actions of maintenance and conservation of our rivers, such as the removal of dead trees and pruning that prevent access to the channel or reduce its capacity or removal of elements dragged by the current that block the channel, especially in works of weight. The fourth and last of the objectives of the Galician Continental Fisheries Act focuses on the importance of management for balanced use of the rivers. In this way, the continental waters are classified as fishables and not fishables. And in fishing we distinguish between free waters for fishing or waters subject to a special regime. Within these last two categories: sports-social and training scenarios and fishing waters for various uses; a measure included in the package of measures to boost fishing tourism in our rivers and which already applies in other communities, such as Castile-Leon. These waters are used to promote fishing, to carry out programmes with children, for the practice of older fishers, to enable the training of competitors and to move the fishing economy in periods of ban, and in particular to benefit the rural environment. The text also provides for a plan for the use and management of continental fisheries through the development of a plan for the management of continental fisheries in the region. As for the species, it sets out a list of which can be the subject of continental fishing and establishes a self-guided regime for the specimens, as is the case with the shark, and for the identification of catches, so that it will be the fisherman himself, which informs the Administration of the data of the catches made. This is something new that exists in other countries of the European Union and is an example of co-responsibility in management by those who practice fishing. In addition, it integrates the regulation of invasive exotic species and regulates restocks and looses, determining that the latter must always be carried out with native species and under the control of the Environmental Council. Finally, and also in the text, a restructuring of infringements is included, and it pays special attention to repairing the damage caused and restoring the changed situation to its previous state. One of the focussing on the new text is the Law against Stealth, tightening sanctions for fishing without license and using prohibited devices, and also in the eradication of invasive exotic species, including the possibility of taking specific measures and determining the prohibition of their return to water. Finally, the use of paralysing or repellent devices and the marketing of any kind of sport or recreational fishing is also prohibited. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, since the Environmental Council, we have continued to work to increase the number of continental fishing licences. We understand that it is necessary to ensure generational relief, and also to encourage new regulations to seek our support for fishermen so that they can develop this practice in the best possible conditions, always looking for sustainable and environmentally friendly fishing. What we want, ultimately, is that our rivers continue to be a source of life along and across our geography, because the rivers of Galicia are our flag at the natural, environmental and also landscape level. They are an indispensable part of our green and blue Galicia. Thank you very much.

It seems a lie, Mr. Arangüena, that you do not know the principle of proportionality, when it so much argues to the law; a principle of proportionality as set out in Article 29 of Law 40/2015, of the legal regime of the public sector, and in whose virtue the imposition of sanctions by public administrations must observe the appropriateness between the sanction to be imposed and the adequacy, of course, to the seriousness of the fact. But, in addition, that proportionality between damage and fine is guaranteed by the need to repair environmental damage, which can be done simultaneously with the sanctioning procedure. And thirdly, it is also necessary to take into account Law 9/2010 of Galician waters, in whose regime of infringements and sanctions of Title VII, the legal good is to ensure the quality of the waters. As far as minor infringements are concerned, they keep their accounts, suppressing their contents in the category of less serious infringements. In addition, it provides that, together with the penalty, the fishing licence may be carried out or withdrawn and the licence is disabled to obtain it for a period of one year. For serious infringements, the minimum and maximum amounts of EUR 3.001 to EUR 25,000 are also virtually equal. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u61	We are now debating the draft law on the management of Galician territory. This bill has been amended to the full of the Socialist Parliamentary Group and the Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block in Wales. For the presentation of the bill, Mrs. Ángeles Vázquez Mejuto has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u62	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to introduce the second of the legislative texts which today bring to the House the Environmental, Territorial and Living Council. After discussing the Continental Fisheries Act, we began the debate on the whole of which will be the new Law on the Management of Galician Territory, restarting the processing initiated in January this year and which was suspended due to the election campaign. We have had this whole debate in Parliament, but I would like to stress again the importance of the text and this text for the Greeks and the Greeks. The future Land Management Act updates the current legislation, which already has twenty-five years old, Law 10/1995. After a quarter of a century in which he has changed and continues to change Galicia and society, our community needs a simpler, up-to-date normative text and, at the same time, an efficient text. The territory is our most precious, the support in which we have developed all our activities, and that is why we need a text that allows us to act with the maximum guarantees and that it bets on the protection of the soil, its conservation, but also its position in value. For all this, the law contains a series of goals that seek to improve the quality of life of the Greeks and the Greeks, such as achieving a balanced development for the whole of the Galician community, ensuring the accessibility of all citizens to all infrastructures and services, improving the relationship of the rural world, rural development as well as urban, betting on sustainable economic development, taking advantage of and valuing the opportunities offered by the territory and protecting the values it presents, both natural and cultural. Hence the importance of this law, which simplifies and quickens urban planning to avoid possible imbalances, improve territorial coordination, which is fundamental. And it follows the path begun with the drafting of the Land Law and its regulation of more simple, useful, efficient and also practical regulations. Without a doubt, the processing of this text is another milestone in the work carried out by the Government of Galicia in the last decade to modernize and update the urban body of our autonomous community, and it will complete the instruments necessary for sustainable and respectful urbanism, adding to the milestones in urbanism achieved in the last decade and which included the Land Management Guidelines, the Land Management Plan, the Land Law and the Development of its Regulation, the Intervention Guides in the Territory, the Landscape Catalogs, the Autonomic Basic Plan, the Municipal Basic Plans, the Landscape Pacts and the Technical Urban Planning Standards. In short, it is a law that responds to three major objectives: no general planning council, facilitates orderly and sustainable development of the natural environment and the protection of the landscape; fundamental. That's why we bet on their participation in their processing, and so in the public information process this text was forwarded to a total of 300 entities. Two hundred and forty-nine allegations were received, of which a total of one hundred and thirteen were estimated, in whole or in part; that is, 45% were estimated. Which allowed the text drafted by the Environmental, Territorial and Living Council to be enriched in this way. The Territorial Management Act sets out four territorial management instruments: the Territorial Management Guidelines, the Territorial Plans... these are divided into integrated and special, the most significant of which are integrated the Coastal Management Plan, the POL, and as a special highlight of those applied to a specific area such as the Eume Integrated Territorial Plan or what we are currently processing from San Fiz de Chantada. Sectoral plans, such as the Plan of Wind or the Plan of Business Areas of Galicia, or projects of self-interest, that replace sectoral projects of supra-municipal impact and regulate the expansion of large companies or large infrastructures, such as the expansion of hospitals. As an example is that of the Coruña or Pontevedra. And they replace sectoral projects of supramunicipal incidence. As far as the guidelines are concerned, they do not vary greatly with regard to the 1995 law; they are instruments of a global nature. It is the general framework of reference that we have on the territory, and it lays down spatial rules for implementation, uses and activities, always in agreement and taking into account the social, economic, cultural and environmental needs. The second of the instruments is territorial plans, which differ between integrated and special, depending on their larger or smaller scale of territorial condition. The integrateds allow them to organize sub-municipal geographic areas with homogeneous characteristics or those which by their size or functional relationship require a planning of land use, productive activities, infrastructure or equipment. And in the case of specials, they provide response and support to special field planning requirements taking into account different characteristics: morphological, agricultural, livestock, forest or even ethnographic requirements, which require unitary consideration. The third of the instruments is the sectoral plans, which include a novelty: it extends its scope to make it the whole autonomous community, and it also establishes its definition with respect to the law of 1995. The goal is to achieve better management of the activities that can be implemented in our territory. It should be noted that these plans can only be of public initiative, the administration that determines where the activities are installed, and that they are redefined as instruments that regulate the implementation of sectoral activities, such as waste management, protected housing or supra-municipal equipment. And finally, there's the fourth management tool that contemplates LOT, which is the projects of self-interest, which replace sectoral projects of supra-municipal impact. This is the only direct intervention instrument in the territory that will serve to plan and execute concrete actions, implement urban appropriations, create soil for protected housing, create soil for economic activities, among others. They must be justified that they transcend the municipal domain because of their territorial, economic, social or even cultural impact, and that they have no prediction or accommodation in the planning of the city. In addition, they can be public or private, and they can develop effective sectoral plans or be autonomous. As a novelty, his statement of environmental impact expires at the age of three. In addition, the law includes the creation of the Territory Planning and Urban Planning Register in Galicia, which sets out the rules of greater legal certainty, transparency and also accessibility for all citizens. In this log, all land management instruments are inserted before their entry into force, thus avoiding a lot of problems that could have occurred before. That is to say, for a management instrument to enter into force will be a requirement for its registration in the said register, so it will be a guarantee of legal security, since we will be before the final version of that instrument, and it will be to which both the administrations and that citizen will have access that way. We also bet on this law for agility and administrative simplification. The new Land Management Act is set by administrative simplification, both in the number of figures and in the course of its processing. In this way, a single processing procedure is established for all land management instruments, distinguishing between those who undergo regular or simplified strategic environmental assessment. The aim is to integrate the environmental procedure into the process of the management instrument, avoiding having to resort to various rules to identify which procedure is applicable to it. This means it's a clearer law, a more useful law, a more practical law, and it makes its application easier. Improving and at the same time giving greater responsibility among different administrations, but giving life to the municipalities and avoiding duplication of proceedings as well as procedures. In short, the new Land Management Act we are discussing today in this plenary aims to correct errors that have been detected during the years of application of the current law, trying to improve it and provide the Community with a useful tool to carry out its management. As it could not be otherwise, this review and update was also used to incorporate into the content of the law criteria such as environmental sustainability, the protection of landscape values, the fight against climate change, the guarantee of universal accessibility of services as well as infrastructures and rather the consideration of the soil as well exhausted and limited. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, we are very aware of the importance of the new Land Management Act for our community. And since June we have argued that urban legislation is a guarantee of success to achieve that the land management of each municipality is coherent and equal for citizens of the municipality itself, but also for citizens of the rest of Galicia, of the contiguous. There is also a need for supra-municipal instruments to guarantee equal opportunities at the level of the autonomous community in order to correct possible imbalances resulting from the variety that our territory presents. That's why the future Land Management Act adapts to Galicia's territorial evolution and its current reality, and presents itself as a way to ensure social cohesion, the improvement in quality of life not only for the present generations, but also for future generations. Nothing else, thank you very much.

And the bill also does not introduce, as usual, any kind of real commitment or future obligation on the part of the Government of Galician Union, with proclaimed objectives that contrast with the amount of public expenditure actually devoted to matters related to the territorial management of Galicia in the last decade. Counsellor, this text does not seem to establish as a guiding principle the right to equality on the part of persons with disabilities through the promotion of personal autonomy and universal accessibility, eradicating any form of discrimination in this regard. As for social and economic cohesion and the perspective of gender, it does not consider essential elements such as interrelationship, proximity and combination of uses to confluence the four spheres of the daily route: production, reproduction, politics and staff, and to give them the same value. Sustainability as an axis that transversally permeates all urbanism that must take care of the environment, water, energy, pollution, soil, waste, natural environment, green areas or natural spaces. It doesn't employ the concept of a city caretaker as an urban model; that is, urban space, mobility, public transport, accessibility, security and equipment are dependent on people and everyday life. Nor does it guarantee the right of information to citizenship and affected groups, nor does it promote citizen participation in all phases, with particular care to include both parity and diversity in all its aspects. As for citizen participation, the process should be instrumentalized through public participation plans, which should contain a number of clear elements, such as the identification of social agents and relevant citizenship, the summary of the most important management proposals, the memory of viability and economic sustainability, the methodology of dissemination and participation tools and so forth. Finally, Counsellor, the Drafting Officer of the Territorial Management Guidelines, we believe that it should be in conjunction with the previous reports and documents that have been used for their elaboration to be forwarded to the Galician Parliament for its knowledge. It's not just a matter of assessing memories of the assessment of urban legislation and its implementation. And finally, we are particularly concerned about the disappearance of the territorial Institute of Studies in the text. It seems necessary for a body which has lent — and here I have many times recognised — a fundamental service to the territorial management of this autonomous community to contain in the text a specific paragraph. So we would like all these aspects that I just pointed out along with others that I don't have time to include in a legal text, which — I repeat — we consider unnecessary. That's why we're going to ask this camera to return the bill to the Government of Galicia. Nothing more and thank you very much.

[[Applause]] And we agree that those self-interest projects can always be positive and when they're associated with a prior planning, but what you're doing in this case is separating them from any kind of planning. We agree that it may be interesting to make public infrastructure, but what you propose with this project of self-interest is to allow private operators to speculate on the ground and use natural resources without any criteria. We therefore insist on our rejection of this bill, especially by projects of self-interest, as defined, but also generally due to the lack of answers, to the fact that we have both the previous legislation and the lack of ability to respond to future challenges. And you were talking here that we were talking about partial amendments. Of course, we are talking about partial amendments, of course we are talking about the amendments we tabled, because the amendments we tabled not only have an opposition to this law, but also have a pro-positive character, of thinking about what is the management of the territory that Galicia needs in 2020. And I want to point out here at least four questions that seem to us the most structural: Firstly, it is necessary to reformulate the role of the Territorial Management Guidelines. The main lesson we have found for the last nine years that the guidelines have been adopted is that although they are an instrument that is located at the top of the pyramid of the computer system, which is the base frame to which the rest of the instruments are then subject, in practice it did not have the ability to influence and operate on planning. Because if later plans that are generally in a bottom line are not approved, the guidelines are reduced to an explanatory book without real application. Legal reform should therefore be geared towards correcting this situation. We put forward the following questions: that DOTs should have the capacity to plan on a proposal basis, to set temporary targets; that the guidelines should define and define the supra-municipal areas, preferably metropolitan and comarcal, which divide and articulate the entire territory, and that they should be developed within a maximum of ten years by integrated territorial plans. In addition, in order to avoid the fragmented view of the territory, guidelines should set for each field a set of specific targets, based on general goals in country key, because there must be a vertical relationship between country objectives and the targets of a region or metropolitan area. Secondly, we believe that a new architecture of planning instruments is needed. This land management legislation belongs to the models that organize under the guidelines two types of fundamental plans: subregional scale plans and sectoral plans. We could also debate long and tense whether this is the best formula or whether it would be more appropriate to move forward towards other models of greater integration with urban and landscape issues, but this would lead to a longer debate. By assuming the framework set here for scale and sectoral plans, the fundamental question is how these two types of plans relate horizontally, and which ultimately define the preponderance of a view of the territory or other. It is clear that you are betting on the preponderance of the sectoral plans by means of the finished facts, although then legally both plans are located at the same level. We understand that, on the contrary, sub-regional scale plans must be those which must be the dominant elements, because they are those that have an integral character of social, economic, environmental and cultural issues for the same area. They are the ones who have the ability to have a polyedric vision, opportunities for action, social needs, endogenous resources and problems to solve. On the other hand, sectoral plans actually have some positive points, such as the ability to encourage and order activities. They also have the ability to set ecological limits, even if they don't want to use them. But, above all, sectoral plans are at risk that they contain a fragmented vision of an activity. And if we don't put binding criteria between sectoral plans and sub-regional scale plans, they end up operating outside the needs of society as a whole. That is why we propose that sectoral plans should be preparatory to integrated territorial plans and that the fundamental weight of implementation should be based on integrated territorial plans, which should be those which should comply with those strategic guidelines of the sectoral plans. Finally, we believe that it is also necessary to create a new figure so that we can govern our territory: plans that affect territorial planning. I think we can agree that there is a malfunction between the territorial management competences, which are exclusive to the Greek Government, and the real capacity that we then have to define the infrastructure, equipment and services projects that affect our territory.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u75	Finish, please.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u76	So -- I'm done, what we're proposing is the creation of a new kind of plan to solve this dysfunction with regard to the powers that the State has in our country. We call for the creation of a plan with an impact on territorial planning, where the Council can plan issues such as the road network as a whole, the competitions of Galicia, but also those of state competence, the railway network as a whole, the airports, the ports, the logistics centres, which we can define from Galicia the fundamental parts for the management of the territory. And that when the State Administration bodies, in the exercise of their powers, want to carry out proceedings in the territory of Greece, they must have a favourable report from the competent council, and in the event that there are discrepancies the agreement should be sought.

Returning to the initiative itself, the Spanish Constitution and the European Charter of Local Self-Government lay down and enshrine the principles of autonomy and financial adequacy of local authorities. The Galician Autonomy Statute provides that income from local authorities will be received through the Autonomous Community of Galicia, which will distribute them in accordance with the legal criteria it establishes, both for state revenue and for unconditional grants. And the Law governing the local bases of regime itself — which, by the way, was amended in 2013 when the People's Party was ruling in the State, Mr. Mariano Rajoy— states that, "for the effectiveness of the constitutionally guaranteed autonomy to local authorities, the legislation of the State and the autonomous communities, which regulates the various sectors of public action according to the constitutional distribution of competences, it must ensure to the Councils their right to intervene in how matters directly affect the circle of their interests, giving them the powers to address the characteristics of the public activity concerned and the management capacity of the local entity, in accordance with the principles of decentralization, proximity, efficiency and efficiency, and with close subjection to the regulation of budgetary stability and financial sustainability". And then Article 105 states that, according to the legislation provided for in Article 5, "will provide local holdings with sufficient resources for the purposes of local authorities". Thus, the principle of financial adequacy implies that the local authorities in Greece should have sufficient means for the performance of the functions conferred on them by the laws, and that they should be fed from taxes and participation in the income of the State and the Autonomous Community of Galicia. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-11-10-DSPG011.u148	President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to make my own request in this House to take account of the decisions we have taken and the actions we have carried out since the Greek Government in the event of the turbidity that presented the Eume River and the impact of this circumstance on water supply to the Pontedeume Town Hall. A serious situation, without palliatives, that I assumed the people of Pontedeme and part of Cabanas were without drinking water for about ten days. An episode in which the Council of Galicia first engaged itself in seeking, on the one hand, to be agile, to be effective and, on the other hand, so that those responsible for succeeding assumed the inescapable consequences of their actions. So it's an appearance -- I think it's a coincidence -- to transfer this House transparency -- as we're always working -- and to tell you about all the actions, actions and measures we've been implementing since Galicia and to explain it to the politicians of this House. First of all, for the tranquility of all the neighbors of Pontedeume - said the director of the Waters of Galicia -, the episode of turbidity at no time assumed or led to the presence of toxicity in the water. The constant analyses carried out, which are, of course, to be carried out, as they see; also the Natural Heritage Technicians of the Environmental Council confirm that no damage has occurred in wildlife or fish mortality, and neither has been detected, as confirmed by the Council of the Sea, variations that caused some type of disease to molluscs or other marine organisms in the river. The results of the sediment samples we take also reveal levels of usual parameters of the area's ecology, and without removing relevance and gravity to the matter, it doesn't seem justified at all that alarmism some people move and what they want to cling to. I can assure you, ladies and gentlemen, that our priority is always to guarantee the health of citizens and the quality of water to our neighbours. I come here with the utmost tranquility, with the utmost confidence and the utmost security of duty accomplished. This does not prevent me, of course, from putting me on the role of many of Pontedeme's neighbors, who have been without water for ten days and with those inconveniences, and regret it, but we move confidence, peace and security. Secondly, we also act promptly, with responsibility and with strength and also with a spirit of collaboration at all times, because they know perfectly well that the direct managers of the supply of water to the population and to guarantee the quality of water to the population are the local authorities — the municipal betrayal. And all these actions we made on foot by the river. The river's foot has been both the Galician Water Technicians — the quality of the waters — and the director of the waters and myself, who have moved to the place. From the very first moment we urged the exploiting company to take the appropriate measures to minimize the turbidity of the Eume River. From the very first moment -- and so I said the same day I moved -- we opened an investigation and, therefore, we acted immediately because the circumstances required it. They all know that water is an essential basic service, and availability and quality must be guaranteed. And it's a competition, an obligation, of local administrations, of public administrations. The Galician Union has an obligation to see that the water that is consumed has guarantees and that quality is met in the parameters set. So for public administrations it's an obligation, and for citizens it's a right to consume quality water, quality parameters, and health guarantees. Therefore, restrictions on water consumption have since forced to act quickly, but it is twice as necessary in the face of an episode as we have seen, in which the neighbors of Pontedeme were left without drinking water. It must be borne in mind that supplies at Pontedeme Town Hall serve 11,000 inhabitants and distributes more than 3,000 cubic metres of water a day. We act, as I said, with consistency, with transparency, but also with responsibility, as a direct consequence of fulfilling our obligations. In the field of affection, we act with diligence and responsibility, because this was what forced the situation. We were in an area that affected the Natural Park of the Strawberries of the Eume, with relevance, with singularity, of environmental protection, and also to preserve marine activity. And we did it, even though sanitation, purification, and, as this case is what we're talking about, water supply to the quality population is a municipal responsibility. In any case, I believe that it is known from everyone that the Board is strongly supporting all local entities in the field of hydraulic infrastructures for optimal management of hydraulic infrastructures. And a good example is precisely that of Pontedeume, where we are dealing with hydraulic infrastructure, financed entirely by the Galician Waters, by the Galician Union, in sanitation — which is also a municipal competition. And it doesn't reveal any secret if I tell you that a good part of the budget of the board I have the honour to run is precisely for that, to ensure and enable the supply, sanitation and purification services to be as effective as possible. Here's the data, ladies and gentlemen: EUR 1,100 million since 2009, 300 supply-related events in 165 municipalities since 2009, and 45 debuggers and therefore 1.7 million Greeks with a debugging system, which means two-thirds of our population. And as I said, we act hard. And we act hard not only to seek solutions — which, of course, also — but also to clear responsibility. At the same time that we detected turbidity — which was related to the bad practice of the exploitation of coronation — he gave direct instructions to the exploiting company to take all relevant measures to achieve that turbidity as soon as possible. As I said, the next step was also the investigation to clarify all the concrete facts that caused the turbidity and impact it on whoever was responsible for that situation. Our position, therefore, is clear and unequivocal that no aggression against the quality of water and the environment can be unpunished. And, ladies and gentlemen, in order to ensure quality supply — which they know is municipal responsibility, in this case of the Pontedeume Council — the Council met at the Council of Galicia in full collaboration. Always, from the very first moment, he was next to the Town Hall of Pontedeme, because it was impossible for him to perform this duty. We've been in direct and permanent contact with the Pontedeme Town Hall and also with the water dealership company there in that county. We have always been involving in our decisions the municipal officials, and consensualizing all the actions and being always proactive in seeking solutions, because it was essential to guarantee the quality of water to all the neighbors of Pontedeume and of Cabanas — which have been supplied with drinking water since that system of exploitation. And we have always been willing to provide technical means, material means, whatever was necessary to solve this problem. This collaboration is extended to all municipalities. And we are doing this, with the common objective of ensuring supply, water quality and environmental quality, in all the municipalities of Galicia that need collaboration. For greater transparency about what happened in the turbulent episode of the Eume River, which took them away for ten days the possibility of supplying water to Pontedeme's neighbours, because I will focus my intervention on explaining for the most part what we've done. And, ladies and gentlemen, these are the facts. In the first days, in October, before the worrying level of turbidity that the river presented, the municipalities were alerted and we worked on different lines of action. One of the first steps — of course, logical — was to strengthen the inspections and analyses of Eume's water to know the source and extent of these changes. The conclusion — part of the obvious turbidity of water that we all know — is that there was neither pH nor oxygen change nor any species mortality. They know that there is already a routine monitoring of the quality of the water, which is marked by the Galician Hydrological Plan. In the Eume's coronation it was routinely doing these analytics at different points of the river and of the coronation, and with varying frequencies. There are three control points — specifically one above the reef and two below the waters. But from the moment we detected this state of turbidity, it was reinforced with an extraordinary surveillance plan, reinforcing, of course, the analyticals and inspections in the coronation and also in the river. There were two control points, with daily analyses across the water column vertically, in different depths, of parameters such as temperature, pH, oxygen, chlorophyll and turbidity. It was also made weekly of metallic substances. And so did they in the river. Therefore, in the choir and in the river we substantially and extraordinarily strengthen all our inspections and analyses. In the river, at five checkpoints, numerous parameters were analyzed daily; and weekly also the solid metal substances. In addition, weekly catalogues of solids were also made for X-ray diffraction to determine the different types of compounds — at three different points: at the exit of the clutch, at the return and at the capture. And it was also done on time in the sediments of the concoction. To these routine and extraordinary controls that we have made, we must add those which have made other departments — of which we have already reported — to those which have held the Health Council, those which have held the Environmental Council and those which have held the Council of the Sea. And also those of the environmental emergency specialists, accompanied by water quality technicians who traveled the river to tell them in detail the values of the measures that were in the river and in the coroner, and to take all necessary measures. On 9 October it was found that levels of turbidity reached parameters that were incompatible with human consumption, and without further delay we communicated this situation to the Council of Pontedeume. And the Pontedeme Town Hall, because it decided to prohibit the use of its supplies for human consumption, a measure that was effective on 10 November. When the Council of Pontedeme adopted this decision, there were already the technicians of the Council of Galicia next to the Council of Pontedeme working on it. And so, given the development of the events and proposals and the data that were being provided by Galician water professionals, the decision to place the containment barriers at the catchment point was adopted immediately, as soon as possible, because it was the most sensitive point, and to protect that point to facilitate the positability and treatment of water for supply. Without assuming an immediate solution, from then on it has contributed to a progressive recovery for supply. Physical-chemical treatments were also applied, in which we had to involve ourselves from the Galician Union to reduce the inlet turbidity of water, and the treatment of the debugger itself to make it fit for human consumption. However, in the face of the persistence of turbidity and the consequences of Pontedeme's supply, on the 13th day - so three days later - we declared the emergency, with an investment already specified and analysed and to be destined for the Pontedeme Town Hall of 1.5 million euros. This emergency could have been declared by the Council of Pontedeme, but we declared it. The Council of Pontedeme -- it's not surprising, it's a local administration -- it has the capacity to declare emergencies and to act immediately and urgently, but we decided it from Galicia's board because we had to act and because we wanted to return supplies to the population as soon as possible. This emergency statement allowed us to act urgently, effectively, swiftly, and to take all the measures we have seen necessary to alleviate the situation and correct the deficiencies detected, especially in the municipal potabilizer, which were the deficiencies that we clearly detected when we analyzed everything that was going on in relation to the water supply to Pontedeume in the municipal potabilizer. We were working there with works that are almost finished. And with all these managements, with this work, with speed, transparency and urgency, we managed to make the water drinkable for consumption and restore water supply to the neighbors of Pontedeme and also from Cabanas. By discount, we wanted to guarantee the quality of water in the Eume River and also at its mouth, avoiding any risk that would endanger water resources and aquatic and terrestrial ecosystems linked to these water masses, and we did it, as at first said, on the foot of the river. We traveled to the technical place of Galician Waters, the director of Galician Waters and myself, on the river foot, to help and to solve this situation as quickly as possible and, in addition, to follow up, because we are transparent and because we communicate in a transparent way, don't doubt it. At the initiative of the Council of Galicia - which could be at the initiative of the Pontedeume Council, but it was not - we created a follow-up commission involving the Council of Pontedeume, the Town Council of Cabanas, the company responsible for managing coronation, quality water technicians, also technical staff of the Environmental Council and the Intecmar. We set up this follow-up commission at the Galician board. On 20th that committee met to assess the situation and decided to lift the restrictions. Why? Because the performances we perform work and we see the parameters of turbidity fall. When you act with urgency, with urgency and with responsibility, you deliver results, as we did at the Galician Union. At the same time, we have opened a sanctioning file to the company which exploits the dam for failing to comply with the requirements made by the Galician Waters to follow the operating directives and plans to exploit the coronation. [[Applause]] So, ladies and gentlemen, we created this follow-up commission so that all the agents involved would be informed on time and offer maximum transparency, because that is how we always work. And on that line, you know, we have, of course, called a new follow-up commission for this same week to figure out everything we're seeing and all the actions we're doing, and that we're next to the Town Hall of Pontedeme in this situation from the first moment and at all times. Since the ban on the use of water for municipal supply, technicians, almost immediately, have seen that the turbidity of the river is a direct consequence of the low level of coronation. This decrease in the level of the coriander water sheet, which is low on a level that we consider to be no longer acceptable, is a clear failure to comply with the rules of exploitation and the plan of exploitation. From the very first moment, the company exploiting the encouragement to take the necessary measures to recover the normal situation of the river to alleviate that turbidity. As a result of the neglect of these requirements and, of course, that we are responsible, as we have seen progressively and on time reporting in this case, we have opened a sanctioning file in relation to the management of the dam, which will also bring this penalty in accordance with the damage caused by the water quality of the river we have found and the supply. As part of this dossier, the company has, of course, been introduced to maintain the level of the water of refreshment in the normal operating parameters and to take measures to reduce the turbidity of the rivers. They're facts that are classified in the Water Law, and of course we comply and make us comply with the Water Law. At this point in time this file will be processed, the allegations that are deemed appropriate and will follow its course, of course, and the scope of the sanction will also be analysed; a consideration that logically will already bear in mind the cost of the actions that were necessary to be carried out and that are still being made to reverse the situation totally in which it reaches the quality of the water, such as the restoration of the supply of water to the population, which was something that we were concerned about. We looked for solutions, and we gave solutions, and in this search for solutions we found that ETAP, the Potable Water Treatment Station of the Pontedeme Town Hall, had important deficiencies. That's what we're saying, and then very quickly. He didn't have raw water reagent mixing cameras, nor physico-chemicals, the decanter, small... with those levels of turbidity, he wasn't prepared to guarantee a quality of the water that deserves the population, because it wasn't really adequate or modernized. We found this problem: that the ETAP of Pontedeme did not have the capacity to ensure compliance with the parameters for the supply of drinking water. It was also concluded, after analyzing different alternatives, that the most agile, most effective alternative that allowed us to make the emergency declaration was to act at the municipal positator station, providing it with the systems needed to debug something that is depurable, because we are in a situation where supply to the population would not have to have cut even one day. It was an extraordinary measure that was taken in the face of exceptional circumstances. The emergency was declared, and the goal was to manage that water and provide quality supplies to the population. The emergency has already been approved by the Board of Trustees, and we're already finishing 100 percent of the drinking water treatment station. The mixing chamber will allow you to optimize all the municipal plant equipment, which is about to end. This Thursday, 12th, it'll be practically finished, and the new equipment will be installed, and next week the drinking water treatment station in Pontedeme will be finished 100 percent. So we modernized Pontedeme's drinking water treatment station in three weeks. Within three weeks an ETAP, a Potable Water Treatment Station, was modernized to ensure the water quality of all Pontedeume neighborhoods and a drinking water treatment station for the present and the future, for the next few decades; a drinking water treatment station that in these five years the mayor of Pontedeume did not do; we fixed it from the Junta of Galicia in three weeks agile, efficient way, and thanks to that emergency statement, because we were at all times next to the neighbors of Pontedeume. [[Applause]] This action is going to add up to others, which we are already doing, which we have financed entirely from the waters of Galicia in terms of sanitation, such as the collector of Pontedeume, to optimize the sanitation system. My presentation I hope will serve as a reflection of all political groups: that there is a manifest difficulty in all municipalities to manage the complexity that has the integral cycle of water, supplies and sanitation. And the municipalities, perhaps those of smaller size, even those of large size, have difficulty exercising this competition. Because it's, of course, a contrasting difficulty, as we saw in Pontedeme Town Hall, that I wasn't able to solve and guarantee the quality of water to the population, to the 11,000 people who supply that supply system. The Eume is another example of the shortcomings, the difficulties of the municipalities to manage water, to give their people quality water and to give them the tranquillity that they can live quietly with the water of their village. But where all its local governments, regardless of their political colour, want to give their citizens the best possible welfare, their goal being to improve the lives of men and women living in their territory. The Spanish Constitution and the European Charter of Local Self-Government establish and establish the principles of autonomy and financial adequacy of local authorities. But the reality is that the Greek municipalities, in order to develop that autonomy collected in the laws and meet those needs of their neighbours, do not count on the economic resources needed to meet them. As you know, the resources of the councils come mainly from the own tax resources; on the other hand from the resources from participation in State taxes, the so-called IEP — which, according to the Board of Auditors, for 2018 was 21.76 % of total resources — and, finally, from participation in taxes in the Autonomous Community, the so-called ICP — which also, according to the latest report for 2018 of the Board of Auditors, represented 10.68 %. Both PIE and PICA are unconditional current transfers that aim to cover the differences between expenditure needs and resources that can be obtained by their own means; that is, to contribute to the achievement of the financial adequacy of local farms. And, of course, it's the larger municipalities that have the highest financial autonomy. Thus, the Council itself, in its General Account Control Report of the local entities of Galicia corresponding to the financial year 2018, once again literally sets out the small budget size of the Greek municipalities, compared with their counterparts from the rest of the state, with income figures and per capita expenditure under the national average in all sections of the population. The People's Party, in its 2016 election program of autonomous elections, literally collected: "It is an obligation to modernize the local public administration to improve its service delivery and continue to provide the local authorities with the best tools to deepen the provision of quality public services, the effectiveness of its management and the proximity to citizenship. —Faijóo, In Galicia yes, 2016—". End of date. But the reality is that over the last four years — i.e. in this last legislation — the Government of Greece has not provided a single additional resource to deepen its provision of quality services that it spoke about in its electoral programme. And on the other hand, it has adopted rules that have increased the powers of the municipalities without bringing the resources necessary to develop them. The Greek Government intended that, at the expense of the scarce resources of the municipalities, and by giving up part of their tax revenue, businesses would be settled in Galicia, forgetting that the exclusive competition of industrial policy belongs to the Autonomous Community. Government Feijóo did not assume as a whole the maintenance costs of primary care centres and health centres — in charge of the municipalities — being exclusively autonomous competition and failing to comply with the local Covenant, signed with the Fegamp in 2006. Government Feijóo again charged the municipalities with the responsibility of taking on an important part of the expenses necessary to prevent the spread of this pandemic. And the government Feijóo didn't increase a single euro by the local cooperation fund. Look, with the Government of Emilio Pérez Touriño in just four years, the Local Cooperation Fund increased by EUR 40 million, reaching almost EUR 120 million. In the government of Nuñez Feijóo this fund does not reach EUR 113 million — exactly EUR 112,972,806. I mean, 7 million euros less than during the Tourin government. President Feijóo is an expert in saying one thing to then do another. He's an expert in asking for responsibilities to other administrations while he forgets to fulfill his own. However, the municipalities, with their scarce resources, acted to meet the needs of their neighbours without looking at who the competition was. They acted to care for the most vulnerable.
